Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"75de2dd9-07b8-4c1e-8acd-63e4ed27e9a6","session_id":"79a2257b-7c38-4aa0-84bb-13ac7f0fb3d1"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01A9hLgsyuPkJSynqwvFGnkZ","type":"tool_result","content":"200\t  // MFA Step-Up (Authenticated — elevates existing session)\n201\t  // ==========================================================================\n202\t\n203\t  /**\n204\t   * IP-2: MFA step-up authentication.\n205\t   *\n206\t   * Called when an authenticated user needs to re-verify identity for\n207\t   * sensitive operations (impersonation, cross-tenant access, billing changes).\n208\t   * Returns new tokens with mfaVerified=true claim.\n209\t   *\n210\t   * SECURITY: Requires a valid access token (user must already be logged in).\n211\t   * The TOTP code is verified against the user's MFA secret.\n212\t   */\n213\t  @SkipTenantGuard()\n214\t  @Mutation(() => AuthPayload, { description: 'MFA step-up: re-verify identity for elevated operations' })\n215\t  async mfaStepUp(\n216\t    @CurrentUser('sub') userId: string,\n217\t    @Args('input') input: MfaStepUpInput,\n218\t    @Context() context: GqlContext,\n219\t  ): Promise<AuthPayload> {\n220\t    const forwarded = context.req?.headers?.['x-forwarded-for'];\n221\t    const ipAddress = context.req?.ip || (Array.isArray(forwarded) ? forwarded[0] : forwarded);\n222\t    const userAgent = context.req?.headers?.['user-agent'] as string | undefined;\n223\t\n224\t    const result = await this.mfaService.verifyStepUp(\n225\t      userId,\n226\t      input.code,\n227\t      ipAddress,\n228\t      userAgent,\n229\t    );\n230\t\n231\t    // Step-up elevates an already-authenticated session; it is not a \"remember\n232\t    // me\" login → session cookie.\n233\t    this.setRefreshTokenCookie(context.res, result.refreshToken, false);\n234\t    return this.stripRefreshToken(result);\n235\t  }\n236\t\n237\t  // ==========================================================================\n238\t  // MFA Login Verification (Public — called during login flow)\n239\t  // ==========================================================================\n240\t\n241\t  /**\n242\t   * Verify MFA code during login flow.\n243\t   *\n244\t   * Called after login returns mfaRequired=true with an mfaToken.\n245\t   * Accepts either a 6-digit TOTP code or a recovery code.\n246\t   * On success, returns full auth tokens.\n247\t   */\n248\t  // SECURITY (SEC-CRITICAL-002): a 6-digit TOTP space MUST be velocity\n249\t  // limited at the service. Keying by the challenge token caps guesses per\n250\t  // login session at 5 — rotating IPs buys an attacker nothing.\n251\t  @RateLimit({\n252\t    name: 'mfa-verify',\n253\t    limit: 5,\n254\t    windowMs: 15 * 60_000,\n255\t    identifier: ({ args }) =>\n256\t      (args?.['input'] as { mfaToken?: string } | undefined)?.mfaToken || undefined,\n257\t  })\n258\t  @Public()\n259\t  @Mutation(() => AuthPayload, { description: 'Verify MFA during login (TOTP or recovery code)' })\n260\t  async verifyMfaLogin(\n261\t    @Args('input') input: VerifyMfaLoginInput,\n262\t    @Context() context: GqlContext,\n263\t  ): Promise<AuthPayload> {\n264\t    const forwarded = context.req?.headers?.['x-forwarded-for'];\n265\t    const ipAddress = context.req?.ip || (Array.isArray(forwarded) ? forwarded[0] : forwarded);\n266\t    const userAgent = context.req?.headers?.['user-agent'] as string | undefined;\n267\t\n268\t    const result = await this.mfaService.verifyMfaLogin(\n269\t      input.mfaToken,\n270\t      input.code,\n271\t      ipAddress,\n272\t      userAgent,\n273\t    );\n274\t\n275\t    // Set refresh token as httpOnly cookie, honoring the rememberMe choice the\n276\t    // user made at the password step (carried through the signed mfaToken).\n277\t    this.setRefreshTokenCookie(context.res, result.refreshToken, result.rememberMe ?? false);\n278\t    return this.stripRefreshToken(result);\n279\t  }\n280\t}\n281\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"79a2257b-7c38-4aa0-84bb-13ac7f0fb3d1","uuid":"15167900-8ec1-495b-a089-305693168fb2","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:18:52.065Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-f70392558d21/apps/auth-service/src/modules/authentication/resolvers/mfa.resolver.ts","content":"  // MFA Step-Up (Authenticated — elevates existing session)\n  // ==========================================================================\n\n  /**\n   * IP-2: MFA step-up authentication.\n   *\n   * Called when an authenticated user needs to re-verify identity for\n   * sensitive operations (impersonation, cross-tenant access, billing changes).\n   * Returns new tokens with mfaVerified=true claim.\n   *\n   * SECURITY: Requires a valid access token (user must already be logged in).\n   * The TOTP code is verified against the user's MFA secret.\n   */\n  @SkipTenantGuard()\n  @Mutation(() => AuthPayload, { description: 'MFA step-up: re-verify identity for elevated operations' })\n  async mfaStepUp(\n    @CurrentUser('sub') userId: string,\n    @Args('input') input: MfaStepUpInput,\n    @Context() context: GqlContext,\n  ): Promise<AuthPayload> {\n    const forwarded = context.req?.headers?.['x-forwarded-for'];\n    const ipAddress = context.req?.ip || (Array.isArray(forwarded) ? forwarded[0] : forwarded);\n    const userAgent = context.req?.headers?.['user-agent'] as string | undefined;\n\n    const result = await this.mfaService.verifyStepUp(\n      userId,\n      input.code,\n      ipAddress,\n      userAgent,\n    );\n\n    // Step-up elevates an already-authenticated session; it is not a \"remember\n    // me\" login → session cookie.\n    this.setRefreshTokenCookie(context.res, result.refreshToken, false);\n    return this.stripRefreshToken(result);\n  }\n\n  // ==========================================================================\n  // MFA Login Verification (Public — called during login flow)\n  // ==========================================================================\n\n  /**\n   * Verify MFA code during login flow.\n   *\n   * Called after login returns mfaRequired=true with an mfaToken.\n   * Accepts either a 6-digit TOTP code or a recovery code.\n   * On success, returns full auth tokens.\n   */\n  // SECURITY (SEC-CRITICAL-002): a 6-digit TOTP space MUST be velocity\n  // limited at the service. Keying by the challenge token caps guesses per\n  // login session at 5 — rotating IPs buys an attacker nothing.\n  @RateLimit({\n    name: 'mfa-verify',\n    limit: 5,\n    windowMs: 15 * 60_000,\n    identifier: ({ args }) =>\n      (args?.['input'] as { mfaToken?: string } | undefined)?.mfaToken || undefined,\n  })\n  @Public()\n  @Mutation(() => AuthPayload, { description: 'Verify MFA during login (TOTP or recovery code)' })\n  async verifyMfaLogin(\n    @Args('input') input: VerifyMfaLoginInput,\n    @Context() context: GqlContext,\n  ): Promise<AuthPayload> {\n    const forwarded = context.req?.headers?.['x-forwarded-for'];\n    const ipAddress = context.req?.ip || (Array.isArray(forwarded) ? forwarded[0] : forwarded);\n    const userAgent = context.req?.headers?.['user-agent'] as string | undefined;\n\n    const result = await this.mfaService.verifyMfaLogin(\n      input.mfaToken,\n      input.code,\n      ipAddress,\n      userAgent,\n    );\n\n    // Set refresh token as httpOnly cookie, honoring the rememberMe choice the\n    // user made at the password step (carried through the signed mfaToken).\n    this.setRefreshTokenCookie(context.res, result.refreshToken, result.rememberMe ?? false);\n    return this.stripRefreshToken(result);\n  }\n}\n","numLines":82,"startLine":200,"totalLines":281}}}

`mutation-missing-role-boundary:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'mutation_missing_role_boundary',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'GraphQL mutation has no method/class role, permission, or guard boundary.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        details: { name },\n      });\n    }\n    if (!isPublic && !hasGuardBoundary && routeDecorators.some((decorator) => decorator !== 'Query')) {\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `controller-missing-guard-boundary:${unit.relativePath}:${line}`,\n        rule: 'controller_missing_guard_boundary',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: unit.relativePath,\n        line,\n        message: 'Route boundary has no explicit public marker or method/class guard boundary.',\n        evidence: [{ path: unit.relativePath, line }],\n        details: { routeDecorators: routeDecorators.sort(), name },\n      });\n    }\n  });\n}\n\nfunction collectServiceGuards(units: readonly SourceUnit[]): ReadonlyMap<string, readonly string[]> {\n  const contexts = new Map<string, readonly string[]>();\n  for (const unit of units) {\n    if (!unit.relativePath.endsWith('/app.module.ts')) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    const guards = new Set<string>();\n    visit(unit.sourceFile, (node) => {\n      if (!ts.isObjectLiteralExpression(node) || !objectHasPropertyText(node, 'provide', 'APP_GUARD')) {\n        return;\n      }\n      for (const property of node.properties) {\n        if (!ts.isPropertyAssignment(property)) {\n          continue;\n        }\n        const name = property.name.getText(unit.sourceFile);\n        if (name !== 'useClass' && name !== 'useFactory' && name !== 'useExisting') {\n          continue;\n        }\n        for (const guard of guardNamesInText(property.initializer.getText(unit.sourceFile))) {\n          guards.add(guard);\n        }\n      }\n    });\n    const serviceRoot = unit.relativePath.replace(/\\/app\\.module\\.ts$/, '');\n    contexts.set(serviceRoot, Array.from(guards).sort());\n  }\n  return contexts;\n}\n\nfunction objectHasPropertyText(node: ts.ObjectLiteralExpression, propertyName: string, expectedText: string): boolean {\n  return node.properties.some((property) => {\n    if (!ts.isPropertyAssignment(property) || property.name.getText() !== propertyName) {\n      return false;\n    }\n    return property.initializer.getText().includes(expectedText);\n  });\n}\n\nfunction guardNamesInText(text: string): string[] {\n  const matches = text.match(/\\b[A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:Guard|AuthGuard)\\b/g) ?? [];\n  return [...new Set(matches)].filter((name) =>\n    /Guard$/.test(name)\n    || ['AuthGuard', 'GqlAuthGuard', 'JwtAuthGuard', 'PlatformAdminGuard', 'ServiceIdentityGuard'].includes(name),\n  );\n}\n\nfunction serviceGuardsForUnit(unit: SourceUnit, context: SecurityContext): readonly string[] {\n  const candidates = [...context.serviceGuards.entries()]\n    .filter(([root]) => unit.relativePath.startsWith(`${root}/`))\n    .sort((a, b) => b[0].length - a[0].length);\n  return candidates[0]?.[1] ?? [];","numLines":300,"startLine":1,"totalLines":531}}}

This names the full role set so the\n132\t * intent \"any authenticated tenant member\" is visible in metadata and\n133\t * testable via ROLES_KEY reflection.\n134\t */\n135\texport const ModuleUserOrHigher = () =>\n136\t  Roles(Role.SUPER_ADMIN, Role.TENANT_ADMIN, Role.MODULE_MANAGER, Role.MODULE_USER);\n137\t\n138\t/**\n139\t * Skip tenant guard decorator - for endpoints that don't require tenant context\n140\t */\n141\texport const SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY = 'skipTenantGuard';\n142\texport const SkipTenantGuard = () => SetMetadata(SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY, true);\n143\t\n144\t/**\n145\t * Public decorator - marks endpoint as publicly accessible.\n146\t * Automatically skips both RolesGuard and TenantGuard, so developers\n147\t * do not need to apply @SkipTenantGuard() separately for public endpoints.\n148\t */\n149\texport const IS_PUBLIC_KEY = 'isPublic';\n150\t// applyDecorators is Nest's canonical combinator for exactly this\n151\t// shape — one decorator stamping multiple metadata keys on either a\n152\t// class or a method — and carries the MethodDecorator & ClassDecorator\n153\t// typing without any hand-rolled target/descriptor bridging.\n154\texport const Public = (): MethodDecorator & ClassDecorator =>\n155\t  applyDecorators(\n156\t    SetMetadata(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, true),\n157\t    SetMetadata(SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY, true),\n158\t  );\n159\t\n160\t/**\n161\t * Check if metadata indicates public access\n162\t */\n163\texport function isPublicMetadata(metadata: Record<string, unknown>): boolean {\n164\t  return metadata[IS_PUBLIC_KEY] === true;\n165\t}\n166\t\n167\t/**\n168\t * Check if metadata indicates tenant guard should be skipped\n169\t */\n170\texport function shouldSkipTenantGuard(metadata: Record<string, unknown>): boolean {\n171\t  return metadata[SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY] === true;\n172\t}\n173\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"79a2257b-7c38-4aa0-84bb-13ac7f0fb3d1","uuid":"5ac26c8a-0212-4ad2-9a93-bdf9652f69d3","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:19:49.125Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-f70392558d21/libs/backend-common/src/decorators/roles.decorator.ts","content":" * must be explicitly role-gated for defense-in-depth (ADR-008) instead of\n * relying on the bare JWT guard. This names the full role set so the\n * intent \"any authenticated tenant member\" is visible in metadata and\n * testable via ROLES_KEY reflection.\n */\nexport const ModuleUserOrHigher = () =>\n  Roles(Role.SUPER_ADMIN, Role.TENANT_ADMIN, Role.MODULE_MANAGER, Role.MODULE_USER);\n\n/**\n * Skip tenant guard decorator - for endpoints that don't require tenant context\n */\nexport const SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY = 'skipTenantGuard';\nexport const SkipTenantGuard = () => SetMetadata(SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY, true);\n\n/**\n * Public decorator - marks endpoint as publicly accessible.\n * Automatically skips both RolesGuard and TenantGuard, so developers\n * do not need to apply @SkipTenantGuard() separately for public endpoints.\n */\nexport const IS_PUBLIC_KEY = 'isPublic';\n// applyDecorators is Nest's canonical combinator for exactly this\n// shape — one decorator stamping multiple metadata keys on either a\n// class or a method — and carries the MethodDecorator & ClassDecorator\n// typing without any hand-rolled target/descriptor bridging.\nexport const Public = (): MethodDecorator & ClassDecorator =>\n  applyDecorators(\n    SetMetadata(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, true),\n    SetMetadata(SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY, true),\n  );\n\n/**\n * Check if metadata indicates public access\n */\nexport function isPublicMetadata(metadata: Record<string, unknown>): boolean {\n  return metadata[IS_PUBLIC_KEY] === true;\n}\n\n/**\n * Check if metadata indicates tenant guard should be skipped\n */\nexport function shouldSkipTenantGuard(metadata: Record<string, unknown>): boolean {\n  return metadata[SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY] === true;\n}\n","numLines":44,"startLine":130,"totalLines":173}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01L1MAAgxaKFqezZhwHbwxtq","type":"tool_result","content":"40\t *   accepting them would allow tenant context spoofing.\n41\t *\n42\t *   **SUPER_ADMIN** — May impersonate a specific tenant only through the\n43\t *   gateway-resolved, HMAC-bound effective-tenant context. Regular subgraphs\n44\t *   consume the verified user assertion; auth-service consumes the verified\n45\t *   gateway service identity because it verifies the user's JWT itself.\n46\t *\n47\t * Skip behaviour:\n48\t * - `@SkipTenantGuard()` skips tenant validation for a single route that\n49\t *   still requires authentication.\n50\t * - `@Public()` marks the endpoint as publicly accessible; TenantGuard\n51\t *   checks both `skipTenantGuard` and `isPublic` metadata so either\n52\t *   decorator is sufficient to bypass tenant validation.\n53\t *\n54\t * SECURITY (H-13): SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access is **persistently** audit-logged\n55\t * via AuditLogService with action `SUPER_ADMIN_CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS`. The audit record\n56\t * includes userId, sourceTenantId, targetTenantId, endpoint, timestamp, client IP,\n57\t * and user agent. An ephemeral logger.warn() is also emitted for real-time observability.\n58\t *\n59\t * MFA Step-Up: SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access requires `mfaVerified: true` in the JWT\n60\t * claims by default (`MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT` defaults to `'true'`).\n61\t * Set `MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT=false` to opt out during MFA rollout.\n62\t */\n63\t@Injectable()\n64\texport class TenantGuard implements CanActivate {\n65\t  private readonly logger = new Logger(TenantGuard.name);\n66\t  private readonly isProduction = process.env['NODE_ENV'] === 'production';\n67\t\n68\t  /** Whether MFA step-up is required for SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access. */\n69\t  private readonly mfaRequiredForCrossTenant: boolean;\n70\t\n71\t  // WHY: Explicit @Inject() — design:paramtypes may not survive all build/runtime environments\n72\t  // (Alpine musl, prod-only deps). Belt-and-suspenders for NestJS DI resolution.\n73\t  // The audit dependency uses the AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE token + IAuditLogService\n74\t  // interface (NOT the concrete class) so this guard never imports the\n75\t  // `AuditLogEntity` decorator, even transitively. Services that wire\n76\t  // `AuditLogModule.forRoot()` provide the token. Production cross-tenant\n77\t  // requests fail closed if that mandatory append capability is unavailable.\n78\t  constructor(\n79\t    @Inject(Reflector) private reflector: Reflector,\n80\t    @Optional() @Inject(AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE) private readonly auditLogService?: IAuditLogService,\n81\t    @Optional() @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService?: ConfigService,\n82\t  ) {\n83\t    this.mfaRequiredForCrossTenant =\n84\t      this.configService?.get<string>('MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT', 'true') !== 'false';\n85\t\n86\t    if (!this.auditLogService) {\n87\t      this.logger.warn(\n88\t        JSON.stringify({\n89\t          event: 'cross_tenant_audit_capability_unavailable',\n90\t          productionFailClosed: this.isProduction,\n91\t        }),\n92\t      );\n93\t    }\n94\t  }\n95\t\n96\t  /**\n97\t   * Evaluate tenant isolation rules for the current request.\n98\t   *\n99\t   * SECURITY (ONEMLI-05): Signature is `async` because cross-tenant audit\n100\t   * logging for SUPER_ADMIN must be awaited to guarantee persistence of\n101\t   * critical security events. NestJS guards fully support both sync\n102\t   * (`boolean`) and async (`Promise<boolean>`) return types.\n103\t   */\n104\t  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n105\t    // Skip if endpoint is marked public\n106\t    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(\n107\t      IS_PUBLIC_KEY,\n108\t      [context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],\n109\t    );\n110\t\n111\t    if (isPublic) {\n112\t      return true;\n113\t    }\n114\t\n115\t    // Skip if explicitly annotated with @SkipTenantGuard()\n116\t    const skipGuard = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(\n117\t      SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY,\n118\t      [context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],\n119\t    );\n120\t\n121\t    if (skipGuard) {\n122\t      return true;\n123\t    }\n124\t\n125\t    const contextType = context.getType<string>();\n126\t    let request: TenantRequest;\n127\t\n128\t    if (contextType === 'graphql') {\n129\t      const gqlCtx = GqlExecutionContext.create(context);"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"79a2257b-7c38-4aa0-84bb-13ac7f0fb3d1","uuid":"182a5b6e-9259-49eb-a651-08406981cf08","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:19:58.358Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-f70392558d21/libs/backend-common/src/guards/tenant.guard.ts","content":" *   accepting them would allow tenant context spoofing.\n *\n *   **SUPER_ADMIN** — May impersonate a specific tenant only through the\n *   gateway-resolved, HMAC-bound effective-tenant context. Regular subgraphs\n *   consume the verified user assertion; auth-service consumes the verified\n *   gateway service identity because it verifies the user's JWT itself.\n *\n * Skip behaviour:\n * - `@SkipTenantGuard()` skips tenant validation for a single route that\n *   still requires authentication.\n * - `@Public()` marks the endpoint as publicly accessible; TenantGuard\n *   checks both `skipTenantGuard` and `isPublic` metadata so either\n *   decorator is sufficient to bypass tenant validation.\n *\n * SECURITY (H-13): SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access is **persistently** audit-logged\n * via AuditLogService with action `SUPER_ADMIN_CROSS_TENANT_ACCESS`. The audit record\n * includes userId, sourceTenantId, targetTenantId, endpoint, timestamp, client IP,\n * and user agent. An ephemeral logger.warn() is also emitted for real-time observability.\n *\n * MFA Step-Up: SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access requires `mfaVerified: true` in the JWT\n * claims by default (`MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT` defaults to `'true'`).\n * Set `MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT=false` to opt out during MFA rollout.\n */\n@Injectable()\nexport class TenantGuard implements CanActivate {\n  private readonly logger = new Logger(TenantGuard.name);\n  private readonly isProduction = process.env['NODE_ENV'] === 'production';\n\n  /** Whether MFA step-up is required for SUPER_ADMIN cross-tenant access. */\n  private readonly mfaRequiredForCrossTenant: boolean;\n\n  // WHY: Explicit @Inject() — design:paramtypes may not survive all build/runtime environments\n  // (Alpine musl, prod-only deps). Belt-and-suspenders for NestJS DI resolution.\n  // The audit dependency uses the AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE token + IAuditLogService\n  // interface (NOT the concrete class) so this guard never imports the\n  // `AuditLogEntity` decorator, even transitively. Services that wire\n  // `AuditLogModule.forRoot()` provide the token. Production cross-tenant\n  // requests fail closed if that mandatory append capability is unavailable.\n  constructor(\n    @Inject(Reflector) private reflector: Reflector,\n    @Optional() @Inject(AUDIT_LOG_SERVICE) private readonly auditLogService?: IAuditLogService,\n    @Optional() @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService?: ConfigService,\n  ) {\n    this.mfaRequiredForCrossTenant =\n      this.configService?.get<string>('MFA_REQUIRED_FOR_CROSS_TENANT', 'true') !== 'false';\n\n    if (!this.auditLogService) {\n      this.logger.warn(\n        JSON.stringify({\n          event: 'cross_tenant_audit_capability_unavailable',\n          productionFailClosed: this.isProduction,\n        }),\n      );\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Evaluate tenant isolation rules for the current request.\n   *\n   * SECURITY (ONEMLI-05): Signature is `async` because cross-tenant audit\n   * logging for SUPER_ADMIN must be awaited to guarantee persistence of\n   * critical security events. NestJS guards fully support both sync\n   * (`boolean`) and async (`Promise<boolean>`) return types.\n   */\n  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n    // Skip if endpoint is marked public\n    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(\n      IS_PUBLIC_KEY,\n      [context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],\n    );\n\n    if (isPublic) {\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    // Skip if explicitly annotated with @SkipTenantGuard()\n    const skipGuard = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(\n      SKIP_TENANT_GUARD_KEY,\n      [context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],\n    );\n\n    if (skipGuard) {\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    const contextType = context.getType<string>();\n    let request: TenantRequest;\n\n    if (contextType === 'graphql') {\n      const gqlCtx = GqlExecutionContext.create(context);","numLines":90,"startLine":40,"totalLines":487}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XLz1JhU5GZgeFjhFwFF6Ar","type":"tool_result","content":"420\t        candidate = candidate.resolve()\n421\t    except OSError:\n422\t        return False, \"artifact_unresolvable\"\n423\t    ledger = _artifact_results_ledger(candidate)\n424\t    if ledger is None:\n425\t        return False, \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\"\n426\t    if not ledger.exists():\n427\t        return False, \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\"\n428\t    recorded: str | None = None\n429\t    for row in load_declared_jsonl(ledger, expected_surface=\"agent_invocation_results\"):\n430\t        output_path = row.get(\"output_path\")\n431\t        output_hash = row.get(\"output_hash\")\n432\t        if not isinstance(output_path, str) or not isinstance(output_hash, str):\n433\t            continue\n434\t        # Rows carry both spellings in the wild: Y6 made new rows\n435\t        # store-relative; older rows (and the live drain that surfaced\n436\t        # this defect) carry the runner's absolute path. Compare by the\n437\t        # file named, never by how the row spelled it.\n438\t        try:\n439\t            row_path = Path(output_path)\n440\t            resolved = (\n441\t                row_path.resolve()\n442\t                if row_path.is_absolute()\n443\t                else (ledger.parent.parent / output_path).resolve()\n444\t            )\n445\t        except OSError:\n446\t            continue\n447\t        if resolved == candidate:\n448\t            recorded = output_hash\n449\t            break\n450\t    if recorded is None:\n451\t        return False, \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\"\n452\t    if not candidate.exists() or not candidate.is_file():\n453\t        return False, \"artifact_missing\"\n454\t    digest = \"sha256:\" + hashlib.sha256(candidate.read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n455\t    if digest != recorded:\n456\t        return False, \"artifact_hash_mismatch\"\n457\t    return True, \"kernel_artifact_verified\"\n458\t\n459\t\n460\tdef _check_agent_ref(\n461\t    ref: str,\n462\t    *,\n463\t    root: Path,\n464\t    errors: list[dict[str, Any]],\n465\t    checked: list[str],\n466\t    allow_self_output: bool = False,\n467\t    allow_kernel_artifacts: bool = False,\n468\t) -> None:\n469\t    # Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n470\t    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n471\t    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n472\t    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n473\t    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n474\t    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n475\t    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n476\t    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n477\t    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n478\t    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n479\t    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n480\t    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n481\t        checked.append(ref)\n482\t        return\n483\t    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n484\t        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n485\t        if admissible:\n486\t            checked.append(ref)\n487\t            return\n488\t        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n489\t            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n490\t            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n491\t            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n492\t            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n493\t            errors.append({\n494\t                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n495\t                \"ref\": ref,\n496\t                \"reason\": reason,\n497\t            })\n498\t            return\n499\t    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n500\t    if parsed is None:\n501\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n502\t        return\n503\t    path, line = parsed\n504\t    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n505\t    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n506\t    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n507\t    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n508\t    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n509\t    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n510\t    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n511\t    try:\n512\t        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n513\t    except _GE as exc:\n514\t        msg = str(exc)\n515\t        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n516\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n517\t        else:\n518\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n519\t                           \"error\": msg})\n520\t        return\n521\t    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n522\t    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n523\t    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n524\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n525\t        return\n526\t    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n527\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n528\t        return\n529\t    checked.append(rel_str)\n530\t    if line is None:\n531\t        return\n532\t    if line <= 0:\n533\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n534\t        return\n535\t    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n536\t    if line > line_count:\n537\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n538\t\n539\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"79a2257b-7c38-4aa0-84bb-13ac7f0fb3d1","uuid":"9b3f7712-42d8-4d9a-9f2f-8307433ce323","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:22:02.571Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-f70392558d21/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/evidence_validator.py","content":" False, \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\"\n    if not ledger.exists():\n        return False, \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\"\n    recorded: str | None = None\n    for row in load_declared_jsonl(ledger, expected_surface=\"agent_invocation_results\"):\n        output_path = row.get(\"output_path\")\n        output_hash = row.get(\"output_hash\")\n        if not isinstance(output_path, str) or not isinstance(output_hash, str):\n            continue\n        # Rows carry both spellings in the wild: Y6 made new rows\n        # store-relative; older rows (and the live drain that surfaced\n        # this defect) carry the runner's absolute path. Compare by the\n        # file named, never by how the row spelled it.\n        try:\n            row_path = Path(output_path)\n            resolved = (\n                row_path.resolve()\n                if row_path.is_absolute()\n                else (ledger.parent.parent / output_path).resolve()\n            )\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n        if resolved == candidate:\n            recorded = output_hash\n            break\n    if recorded is None:\n        return False, \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\"\n    if not candidate.exists() or not candidate.is_file():\n        return False, \"artifact_missing\"\n    digest = \"sha256:\" + hashlib.sha256(candidate.read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n    if digest != recorded:\n        return False, \"artifact_hash_mismatch\"\n    return True, \"kernel_artifact_verified\"\n\n\ndef _check_agent_ref(\n    ref: str,\n    *,\n    root: Path,\n    errors: list[dict[str, Any]],\n    checked: list[str],\n    allow_self_output: bool = False,\n    allow_kernel_artifacts: bool = False,\n) -> None:\n    # Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n        checked.append(ref)\n        return\n    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n        if admissible:\n            checked.append(ref)\n            return\n        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n            errors.append({\n                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n                \"ref\": ref,\n                \"reason\": reason,\n            })\n            return\n    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n    if parsed is None:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n        return\n    path, line = parsed\n    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n    try:\n        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n    except _GE as exc:\n        msg = str(exc)\n        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n        else:\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n                           \"error\": msg})\n        return\n    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n        return\n    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n        return\n    checked.append(rel_str)\n    if line is None:\n        return\n    if line <= 0:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n        return\n    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n    if line > line_count:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n\n","numLines":120,"startLine":420,"totalLines":774}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NXZbDonNMHGdqcMZ3GFK4K","type":"tool_result","content":"55\t        return fallback\n56\t    normalized = value.strip().lower()\n57\t    if normalized in FEEDBACK_SEVERITIES:\n58\t        return normalized\n59\t    if normalized == \"informational\":\n60\t        return \"low\"\n61\t    return fallback\n62\t\n63\t\n64\tdef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n65\t    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n66\t    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n67\t    \"\"\"\n68\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n69\t        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n70\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n71\t        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n72\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n73\t        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n74\t        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n75\t        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n76\t        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n77\t        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n78\t        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n79\t        # stays the only authority on validity.\n80\t        return {\n81\t            key: details.get(key)\n82\t            for key in (\n83\t                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n84\t                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n85\t                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n86\t            )\n87\t            if details.get(key) is not None\n88\t        }\n89\t    return {}\n90\t\n91\t\n92\tdef validate_judge_response(\n93\t    *,\n94\t    request: dict[str, Any],\n95\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n96\t) -> list[str]:\n97\t    \"\"\"Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — the judge output contract, checkable BEFORE accept.\n98\t\n99\t    The second sealed night accepted 12 judge results whose envelopes never\n100\t    carried a verdict the bridge could read (\"expected verdict ..., got\n101\t    None\") — each one an accepted row that could never fold, burning bridge\n102\t    retries toward permanent_fail. This is the SINGLE definition of what a\n103\t    judge response must carry; ``record_judge_verdict_from_response`` raises\n104\t    on exactly this list, and the executor's pre-submit gate releases the\n105\t    claim on it instead of sealing an unfoldable result.\n106\t\n107\t    Returns [] for non-judge roles: the contract binds judges only.\n108\t    \"\"\"\n109\t    role = response.get(\"role\")\n110\t    if not is_judge_role(role):\n111\t        return []\n112\t    errors: list[str] = []\n113\t    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n114\t    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n115\t        details = {}\n116\t    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n117\t    if not verdict_block:\n118\t        return [\"judge_verdict:absent\"]\n119\t    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n120\t    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n121\t        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's contract says \"return\n122\t        # details.consensus, OR the uncertainty reason when the consensus\n123\t        # gate cannot be met\", and the deterministic engine it mirrors\n124\t        # emits exactly that non-verdict outcome. The live arbiter obeyed\n125\t        # and this contract burned the claim as invalid:None — fourth\n126\t        # mint-vs-law instance. Uncertainty is a VALID arbitration\n127\t        # outcome, for the arbiter alone, with a reason from the closed\n128\t        # vocabulary; binary judges stay bound to binary verdicts.\n129\t        reason = verdict_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")\n130\t        if (\n131\t            role == \"consensus_arbitration\"\n132\t            and verdict is None\n133\t            and reason in CONSENSUS_UNCERTAINTY_REASONS\n134\t        ):\n135\t            pass\n136\t        else:\n137\t            errors.append(f\"judge_verdict.verdict:invalid:{verdict!r}\")\n138\t    tool_id = request.get(\"tool_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"tool_id\")\n139\t    run_id = request.get(\"run_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"run_id\")\n140\t    finding_id = request.get(\"finding_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_id\")\n141\t    if not (tool_id and run_id and finding_id):\n142\t        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:missing_tool_run_or_finding_id\")\n143\t    judge_id = details.get(\"agent_subagent_type\") or verdict_block.get(\"judge_id\")\n144\t    if not judge_id:\n145\t        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:missing_judge_identity\")\n146\t    elif str(judge_id).startswith(\"ci-executor:\"):\n147\t        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:executor_shaped_judge_identity\")\n148\t    return errors\n149\t\n150\t\n151\tdef record_judge_verdict_from_response(\n152\t    *,\n153\t    request: dict[str, Any],\n154\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n155\t    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n156\t) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n157\t    \"\"\"Translate an ACCEPTED judge response into an ai_judge feedback row.\n158\t\n159\t    Returns the persisted row, or None when the envelope role is not a\n160\t    judge role (the bridge is a no-op for non-judge submissions).\n161\t\n162\t    Raises GovernanceError when the response is missing the data the\n163\t    consensus engine requires (tool_id / run_id / finding_id come from\n164\t    the request envelope's optional fields; verdict + severity from\n165\t    the response.details).\n166\t    \"\"\"\n167\t    role = response.get(\"role\")\n168\t    if not is_judge_role(role):\n169\t        return None\n170\t\n171\t    # Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — one contract, two consumers: the executor's\n172\t    # pre-submit gate checks exactly this list and releases instead of\n173\t    # sealing an unfoldable accepted result; the bridge raises on it so a\n174\t    # row that somehow slipped past still fails loudly, never half-folds.\n175\t    contract_errors = validate_judge_response(request=request, response=response)\n176\t    if contract_errors:\n177\t        raise GovernanceError(\n178\t            \"judge bridge contract violation: \" + \"; \".join(contract_errors)\n179\t        )\n180\t\n181\t    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n182\t    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n183\t        details = {}\n184\t\n185\t    # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's legitimate non-verdict outcome\n186\t    # folds through the SAME producer, ledger and idempotent escalation_id\n187\t    # as the deterministic engine, so one sweep drains both lanes into one\n188\t    # HUMAN_REQUIRED record. It is NOT a feedback verdict: an uncertain\n189\t    # arbitration must never write an ai_judge row.\n190\t    if role == \"consensus_arbitration\":\n191\t        arb_block = _verdict_field(details)\n192\t        arb_reason = arb_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")\n193\t        if arb_block.get(\"verdict\") is None and arb_reason in CONSENSUS_UNCERTAINTY_REASONS:\n194\t            from .feedback_store import record_consensus_uncertainty\n195\t\n196\t            row = record_consensus_uncertainty(\n197\t                tool_id=str(request.get(\"tool_id\") or arb_block.get(\"tool_id\") or \"\"),\n198\t                run_id=str(request.get(\"run_id\") or arb_block.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n199\t                finding_id=str(request.get(\"finding_id\") or arb_block.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n200\t                group_id=str(request.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or arb_block.get(\"group_id\") or \"\"),\n201\t                reason=str(arb_reason),\n202\t                cycle_id=request.get(\"cycle_id\"),\n203\t                base_dir=base_dir,\n204\t            )\n205\t            return {\"kind\": \"consensus_uncertainty\", **row}\n206\t    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n207\t\n208\t    tool_id = request.get(\"tool_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"tool_id\")\n209\t    run_id = request.get(\"run_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"run_id\")\n210\t    finding_id = request.get(\"finding_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_id\")\n211\t    if not (tool_id and run_id and finding_id):\n212\t        raise GovernanceError(\n213\t            \"judge bridge requires tool_id, run_id, finding_id in request or response.details.verdict; \"\n214\t            f\"got tool_id={tool_id!r}, run_id={run_id!r}, finding_id={finding_id!r}\"\n215\t        )\n216\t\n217\t    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n218\t    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n219\t        raise GovernanceError(\n220\t            f\"judge bridge expected verdict in {FEEDBACK_VERDICTS}, got {verdict!r}\"\n221\t        )\n222\t    severity = _coerce_severity(verdict_block.get(\"severity\") or details.get(\"severity\"))\n223\t    # D1 (Kapalı Döngü) — judge identity. `response[\"agent_id\"]` is the\n224\t    # EXECUTOR's identity (`ci-executor:gha-<run>`), not the judge's: reading\n225\t    # it collapsed every judge drained by one workflow run into a single\n226\t    # voter (consensus structurally impossible) while judges drained by\n227\t    # DIFFERENT runs counted as distinct voters with meaningless identity.\n228\t    # The judge's real identity is the subagent the executor invoked\n229\t    # (`details.agent_subagent_type`, force-stamped by ci_executor); the\n230\t    # mock lane and legacy envelopes carry it as `verdict.judge_id`.\n231\t    judge_id = details.get(\"agent_subagent_type\") or verdict_block.get(\"judge_id\")\n232\t    if not judge_id:\n233\t        raise GovernanceError(\n234\t            \"judge bridge requires details.agent_subagent_type \"\n235\t            \"(or details.verdict.judge_id)\"\n236\t        )\n237\t    if str(judge_id).startswith(\"ci-executor:\"):\n238\t        # Refuse loudly instead of silently repairing: an executor-shaped\n239\t        # identity means the producer regressed, and a repaired row would\n240\t        # hide that while still poisoning per-judge calibration.\n241\t        raise GovernanceError(\n242\t            f\"judge bridge refuses executor-shaped judge identity: {judge_id!r}\"\n243\t        )\n244\t\n245\t    # The unit-interval gate, not a bare isinstance: True is an int and\n246\t    # float(\"nan\") is a float to isinstance, and both would ride a judge\n247\t    # verdict into calibration as if they were probabilities.\n248\t    confidence = confidence_in_unit_interval(verdict_block.get(\"confidence\"))\n249\t\n250\t    note = (\n251\t        verdict_block.get(\"rationale\")\n252\t        or verdict_block.get(\"note\")\n253\t        or response.get(\"rationale\")\n254\t        or \"envelope-routed judge submission\"\n255\t    )\n256\t    if not isinstance(note, str) or not note.strip():\n257\t        note = \"envelope-routed judge submission\"\n258\t\n259\t    raw_evidence_refs = verdict_block.get(\"evidence_refs\") or response.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n260\t    evidence_refs = [str(r) for r in raw_evidence_refs if isinstance(r, str) and r.strip()]\n261\t\n262\t    row = record_operator_feedback(\n263\t        tool_id=str(tool_id),\n264\t        run_id=str(run_id),\n265\t        finding_id=str(finding_id),\n266\t        verdict=verdict,\n267\t        severity=severity,\n268\t        note=note,\n269\t        source_type=\"ai_judge\",\n270\t        judge_id=str(judge_id),\n271\t        # ORPHAN-HIGH-781 — mint-first doctrine, same as judgment_group_id\n272\t        # and finding_fingerprint above: the model the DISPATCHER resolved\n273\t        # (`details.agent_dispatch_model`, force-stamped by ci_executor from\n274\t        # the runtime profile) outranks the judge's own verdict.model\n275\t        # self-report. The anchor distinct-model count must not trust a\n276\t        # string the judged agent wrote about itself; the self-report\n277\t        # survives only as the fallback for mock/legacy envelopes that\n278\t        # predate the stamp.\n279\t        model=details.get(\"agent_dispatch_model\") or verdict_block.get(\"model\"),"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"79a2257b-7c38-4aa0-84bb-13ac7f0fb3d1","uuid":"3e8669ef-8676-44d2-ae1f-502ba8662a0a","timestamp":"2026-09-19T13:23:09.174Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-f70392558d21/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/judgment_bridge.py","content":"        return fallback\n    normalized = value.strip().lower()\n    if normalized in FEEDBACK_SEVERITIES:\n        return normalized\n    if normalized == \"informational\":\n        return \"low\"\n    return fallback\n\n\ndef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n        # stays the only authority on validity.\n        return {\n            key: details.get(key)\n            for key in (\n                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n            )\n            if details.get(key) is not None\n        }\n    return {}\n\n\ndef validate_judge_response(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — the judge output contract, checkable BEFORE accept.\n\n    The second sealed night accepted 12 judge results whose envelopes never\n    carried a verdict the bridge could read (\"expected verdict ..., got\n    None\") — each one an accepted row that could never fold, burning bridge\n    retries toward permanent_fail. This is the SINGLE definition of what a\n    judge response must carry; ``record_judge_verdict_from_response`` raises\n    on exactly this list, and the executor's pre-submit gate releases the\n    claim on it instead of sealing an unfoldable result.\n\n    Returns [] for non-judge roles: the contract binds judges only.\n    \"\"\"\n    role = response.get(\"role\")\n    if not is_judge_role(role):\n        return []\n    errors: list[str] = []\n    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n        details = {}\n    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n    if not verdict_block:\n        return [\"judge_verdict:absent\"]\n    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's contract says \"return\n        # details.consensus, OR the uncertainty reason when the consensus\n        # gate cannot be met\", and the deterministic engine it mirrors\n        # emits exactly that non-verdict outcome. The live arbiter obeyed\n        # and this contract burned the claim as invalid:None — fourth\n        # mint-vs-law instance. Uncertainty is a VALID arbitration\n        # outcome, for the arbiter alone, with a reason from the closed\n        # vocabulary; binary judges stay bound to binary verdicts.\n        reason = verdict_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")\n        if (\n            role == \"consensus_arbitration\"\n            and verdict is None\n            and reason in CONSENSUS_UNCERTAINTY_REASONS\n        ):\n            pass\n        else:\n            errors.append(f\"judge_verdict.verdict:invalid:{verdict!r}\")\n    tool_id = request.get(\"tool_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"tool_id\")\n    run_id = request.get(\"run_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"run_id\")\n    finding_id = request.get(\"finding_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_id\")\n    if not (tool_id and run_id and finding_id):\n        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:missing_tool_run_or_finding_id\")\n    judge_id = details.get(\"agent_subagent_type\") or verdict_block.get(\"judge_id\")\n    if not judge_id:\n        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:missing_judge_identity\")\n    elif str(judge_id).startswith(\"ci-executor:\"):\n        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:executor_shaped_judge_identity\")\n    return errors\n\n\ndef record_judge_verdict_from_response(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Translate an ACCEPTED judge response into an ai_judge feedback row.\n\n    Returns the persisted row, or None when the envelope role is not a\n    judge role (the bridge is a no-op for non-judge submissions).\n\n    Raises GovernanceError when the response is missing the data the\n    consensus engine requires (tool_id / run_id / finding_id come from\n    the request envelope's optional fields; verdict + severity from\n    the response.details).\n    \"\"\"\n    role = response.get(\"role\")\n    if not is_judge_role(role):\n        return None\n\n    # Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — one contract, two consumers: the executor's\n    # pre-submit gate checks exactly this list and releases instead of\n    # sealing an unfoldable accepted result; the bridge raises on it so a\n    # row that somehow slipped past still fails loudly, never half-folds.\n    contract_errors = validate_judge_response(request=request, response=response)\n    if contract_errors:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge contract violation: \" + \"; \".join(contract_errors)\n        )\n\n    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n        details = {}\n\n    # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's legitimate non-verdict outcome\n    # folds through the SAME producer, ledger and idempotent escalation_id\n    # as the deterministic engine, so one sweep drains both lanes into one\n    # HUMAN_REQUIRED record. It is NOT a feedback verdict: an uncertain\n    # arbitration must never write an ai_judge row.\n    if role == \"consensus_arbitration\":\n        arb_block = _verdict_field(details)\n        arb_reason = arb_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")\n        if arb_block.get(\"verdict\") is None and arb_reason in CONSENSUS_UNCERTAINTY_REASONS:\n            from .feedback_store import record_consensus_uncertainty\n\n            row = record_consensus_uncertainty(\n                tool_id=str(request.get(\"tool_id\") or arb_block.get(\"tool_id\") or \"\"),\n                run_id=str(request.get(\"run_id\") or arb_block.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_id=str(request.get(\"finding_id\") or arb_block.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n                group_id=str(request.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or arb_block.get(\"group_id\") or \"\"),\n                reason=str(arb_reason),\n                cycle_id=request.get(\"cycle_id\"),\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            )\n            return {\"kind\": \"consensus_uncertainty\", **row}\n    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n\n    tool_id = request.get(\"tool_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"tool_id\")\n    run_id = request.get(\"run_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"run_id\")\n    finding_id = request.get(\"finding_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_id\")\n    if not (tool_id and run_id and finding_id):\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge requires tool_id, run_id, finding_id in request or response.details.verdict; \"\n            f\"got tool_id={tool_id!r}, run_id={run_id!r}, finding_id={finding_id!r}\"\n        )\n\n    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            f\"judge bridge expected verdict in {FEEDBACK_VERDICTS}, got {verdict!r}\"\n        )\n    severity = _coerce_severity(verdict_block.get(\"severity\") or details.get(\"severity\"))\n    # D1 (Kapalı Döngü) — judge identity. `response[\"agent_id\"]` is the\n    # EXECUTOR's identity (`ci-executor:gha-<run>`), not the judge's: reading\n    # it collapsed every judge drained by one workflow run into a single\n    # voter (consensus structurally impossible) while judges drained by\n    # DIFFERENT runs counted as distinct voters with meaningless identity.\n    # The judge's real identity is the subagent the executor invoked\n    # (`details.agent_subagent_type`, force-stamped by ci_executor); the\n    # mock lane and legacy envelopes carry it as `verdict.judge_id`.\n    judge_id = details.get(\"agent_subagent_type\") or verdict_block.get(\"judge_id\")\n    if not judge_id:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge requires details.agent_subagent_type \"\n            \"(or details.verdict.judge_id)\"\n        )\n    if str(judge_id).startswith(\"ci-executor:\"):\n        # Refuse loudly instead of silently repairing: an executor-shaped\n        # identity means the producer regressed, and a repaired row would\n        # hide that while still poisoning per-judge calibration.\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            f\"judge bridge refuses executor-shaped judge identity: {judge_id!r}\"\n        )\n\n    # The unit-interval gate, not a bare isinstance: True is an int and\n    # float(\"nan\") is a float to isinstance, and both would ride a judge\n    # verdict into calibration as if they were probabilities.\n    confidence = confidence_in_unit_interval(verdict_block.get(\"confidence\"))\n\n    note = (\n        verdict_block.get(\"rationale\")\n        or verdict_block.get(\"note\")\n        or response.get(\"rationale\")\n        or \"envelope-routed judge submission\"\n    )\n    if not isinstance(note, str) or not note.strip():\n        note = \"envelope-routed judge submission\"\n\n    raw_evidence_refs = verdict_block.get(\"evidence_refs\") or response.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n    evidence_refs = [str(r) for r in raw_evidence_refs if isinstance(r, str) and r.strip()]\n\n    row = record_operator_feedback(\n        tool_id=str(tool_id),\n        run_id=str(run_id),\n        finding_id=str(finding_id),\n        verdict=verdict,\n        severity=severity,\n        note=note,\n        source_type=\"ai_judge\",\n        judge_id=str(judge_id),\n        # ORPHAN-HIGH-781 — mint-first doctrine, same as judgment_group_id\n        # and finding_fingerprint above: the model the DISPATCHER resolved\n        # (`details.agent_dispatch_model`, force-stamped by ci_executor from\n        # the runtime profile) outranks the judge's own verdict.model\n        # self-report. The anchor distinct-model count must not trust a\n        # string the judged agent wrote about itself; the self-report\n        # survives only as the fallback for mock/legacy envelopes that\n        # predate the stamp.\n        model=details.get(\"agent_dispatch_model\") or verdict_block.get(\"model\"),","numLines":225,"startLine":55,"totalLines":388}}}
